{"parentUuid":"2e0b7ef0-104c-4465-bead-56d549353a08","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the Without an answer to that, the two models\n1293\tcan argue forever, and the run never ends — and because checking prose is a\n1294\tcorrectness step, running out of money cannot stop it either.*\n1295\t\n1296\t**The loop that made this necessary.** Composition writes \"roughly 15%\" so a\n1297\tstranger can read it. The judge rejects the softener because the bundle says\n1298\t`0.148`. Composition uses the exact figure. The stranger law rejects a bare number\n1299\tin the top layer. Composition adds explaining prose. The judge rejects the\n1300\tunbundled claim. **Loop.**\n1301\t\n1302\t**Requirement S-4 — gate order** `RULED(DR-049)` · The serve gates run in **exactly\n1303\tthis order**, and the order is not an implementation preference:\n1304\t\n1305\t> **R9 (stranger) → Q53 (objection visibility) → conformance → Q51 (provenance)**\n1306\t\n1307\t**Requirement S-4a — R9 runs on BOTH surfaces** `RULED(DR-057)` · The answer exists\n1308\tin two forms, and **each gets its own stranger check**:\n1309\t\n1310\t1. **Node text, pre-compose** — checked in the gate order above, before any prose\n1311\t   is written. Its result binds the conformance judge (S-5).\n1312\t2. **The composed verdict, post-compose** — the sentence the reader actually reads\n1313\t   gets **its own R9 pass** after composition.\n1314\t\n1315\t**A verdict-R9 failure takes DR-049's existing terminal**: components-only with a\n1316\tDEFECT badge. **It does not open a new recompose loop** — the composed verdict\n1317\talready had its two attempts, and a third would be the loop DR-049 exists to\n1318\tprevent.\n1319\t\n1320\t**Requirement S-4b — conformance sampling scope** `RULED(DR-060)` ·\n1321\t**Load-bearing sentences are always judged.** Non-load-bearing text is sampled at\n1322\tthe **frozen stranger rate** (DR-052). The protected core forbids skipping the\n1323\tconformance **role**; it does **not** mandate exhaustive sampling. Skipping the\n1324\tjudge entirely is prohibited; sampling within the declared rate is not a skip.\n1325\t\n1326\t**Requirement S-5 — the conformance judge is subordinate to the stranger law**\n1327\t`RULED(DR-049)` · **The conformance judge may never demand an edit that violates\n1328\tR9.** R9 runs first and its result binds; a conformance instruction that would make\n1329\ta load-bearing node unreadable is itself rejected. This is what breaks the loop\n1330\tabove at its first turn rather than its third.\n1331\t\n1332\t**Requirement S-6 — Q53's residual is data, not prose** `RULED(DR-049)` · The\n1333\tresidual objection is a **fact-bundle field**. It cannot be satisfied by the\n1334\tcomposition model happening to mention it, and conformance cannot pass a text that\n1335\tomits it while judging only the sentences it did write.\n1336\t\n1337\t**Requirement S-7 — termination** `RULED(DR-049)` · **`max_recompose = 2`.** After\n1338\tthe **second** conformance failure the answer **serves COMPONENTS-ONLY** — the\n1339\tverified facts, the badges and the node graph — **with a visible DEFECT badge**.\n1340\t**Never blank, and never unchecked prose.**\n1341\t\n1342\t| State | Trigger | Next |\n1343\t|---|---|---|\n1344\t| `COMPOSE` | fact bundle assembled and gated (R9, Q53 pass) | → `CONFORM` |\n1345\t| `CONFORM` | conformance judge runs | pass → `PROVENANCE`; fail → `RECOMPOSE` |\n1346\t| `RECOMPOSE` | conformance failure, attempt count < 2 | → `COMPOSE` (attempt +1) |\n1347\t| `PROVENANCE` | Q51 gates run on the text that will ship | pass → `SERVE`; fail → `COMPONENTS_ONLY` |\n1348\t| `COMPONENTS_ONLY` | second conformance failure, **or** a blocking gate that prose cannot repair | → `SERVE_DEGRADED`: verified facts + badges + node graph + **DEFECT badge** |\n1349\t| `SERVE` / `SERVE_DEGRADED` | terminal | the reader always gets something honest |\n1350\t\n1351\t**Requirement S-8** `RULED(DR-049)` · There is **no blocked-and-silent terminal**.\n1352\tEvery path out of SERVE puts something in front of the reader, and every degraded\n1353\tpath says on its face that it is degraded.\n1354\t\n1355\t**Requirement S-9 — fixtures** `CARRIED-DESIGN` · Each terminal needs a fixture:\n1356\ta node-text R9 failure, a **verdict R9 failure**, a Q53 failure, a first\n1357\tconformance failure that recomposes to a pass, a second conformance failure that\n1358\treaches components-only, and a Q51 provenance failure. A state machine with an\n1359\tuntested terminal is a state machine with an unknown terminal.\n1360\t\n1361\t### 12.1b The compose size law — what happens when the facts do not fit (DR-058)\n1362\t\n1363\t*In plain words: the stranger law puts every load-bearing node's restatement into\n1364\tthe material the writing model has to honour. On a big question that material can\n1365\texceed what one pass can hold. The dangerous failure is not running out of room —\n1366\tit is running out of room quietly, and dropping the objection nobody wanted\n1367\tprinted.*\n1368\t\n1369\t**Requirement S-9a — multi-pass composition** `RULED(DR-058)` · An oversized fact\n1370\tbundle is composed in **multiple passes, ordered by load-bearing priority**\n1371\t(summarize, then refine). The bundle is never silently trimmed to fit one pass.\n1372\t\n1373\t**Requirement S-9b — honesty fields are machine-injected** `RULED(DR-058)` ·\n1374\t**Residual objections, badges and condition marks are injected into the output\n1375\tstructure by the machine, outside the composition model's discretion.** They are\n1376\tnot things the model can choose to include; they are fields it composes *around*.\n1377\t**Silent truncation of an honesty surface is therefore impossible by construction**\n1378\t— the model cannot omit what it was never given the option to omit.\n1379\t\n1380\t**Requirement S-9c — the hard budget terminal** `RULED(DR-058)` · Past the\n1381\t**declared** hard bundle budget, the answer takes the components-only terminal\n1382\trather than composing from a partial bundle. A composed answer that silently rests\n1383\ton some of the facts is worse than an uncomposed answer that rests on all of them.\n1384\t\n1385\t### 12.1c Degraded mode still owes the reader the honest parts (DR-059)\n1386\t\n1387\t*In plain words: when composition fails and the engine falls back to components,\n1388\ttwo of the honesty surfaces are sentence-shaped — the reversal point that says which\n1389\tway a value judgement would flip the answer, and the disclosure that this builds on\n1390\ta previous answer. Without prose, those would simply vanish exactly when the reader\n1391\tmost needs them.*\n1392\t\n1393\t**Requirement S-9d — projection fields for the sentence-shaped surfaces**\n1394\t`RULED(DR-059)` · The **reversal point** and the **builds-on-previous disclosure**\n1395\teach get a **structured projection field that renders without composed prose**. In\n1396\tdegraded mode they are **served as data**, not dropped.\n1397\t\n1398\t**Requirement S-9e — replay eviction, not whole-answer refusal** `RULED(DR-059)` ·\n1399\tThe replay law refuses to serve a number that cannot be recomputed. When one\n1400\tcomponent number fails replay, **that number is EVICTED and replaced with a typed\n1401\tmissing-number mark**; **the rest of the answer serves, with a DEFECT badge**.\n1402\t**One number is lost, never the answer.** Refusing the whole answer over one\n1403\tunreplayable component would convert a narrow integrity failure into a total\n1404\toutage, and would give the reader nothing to check.\n1405\t\n1406\t### 12.2 What the machine owns absolutely at serve time\n1407\t\n1408\tThese are **blocking gates**, listed in DR-049's execution order.\n1409\t\n1410\t| Order | Gate | Row | Behaviour |\n1411\t|---:|---|---|---|\n1412\t| 1 | **Stranger-test status** | R9 | Every node **and** the verdict must be individually restatable against the canonical schema (§12.6); the check is mechanical and **blocks serving**. Its result **binds the conformance judge**. |\n1413\t| 2 | **Hidden-objection gate** | Q53 | An endorsed band **may not be served** while an unresolved high-strength counterargument is carried and hidden. The residual is a **fact-bundle field**. |\n1414\t| 3 | **Conformance** | DR-044 | Text↔facts. Mismatch → recompose (max 2) → components-only with a DEFECT badge. **Never budget-skippable.** |\n1415\t| 4 | **Provenance join** | Q51 | Every load-bearing claim joins to its **kind (looked-up / ran / reasoned), producer and locator**. |\n1416\t| 4 | **Locator gate** | Q51 | **A missing locator blocks serving.** |\n1417\t| 4 | **Reasoning-only downgrade** | Q51 | A verdict resting on reasoning alone is **downgraded from a verdict to a hypothesis plus a research plan**. |\n1418\t| — | **Abstention typing** | Q55 | Exactly one of the five kinds **per ignorance-ledger unknown**, consistent with the ledger (§12.3). |"}]},"uuid":"178fce34-e9d8-4732-9a8f-e970549692ab","timestamp":"2026-08-08T13:05:36.369Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4734436f-2cac-440a-b70b-3bf414c1bf64","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{"parentUuid":"fa86b2af-8ec4-4964-b400-9f0c32601675","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1b24d198-6edd-416c-934d-0ab6c293f093","agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017uDP7f4vK6oPWevMs29CKj","type":"tool_result","content":"13 derived-only rows). [ADR-0006/0007; AC-88]\n\n**P6 · CQRS-lite: sealed artifacts vs read-time projections (Seam D).** The\nwrite side seals frozen artifacts (fact bundle, conformance record, served\nnumbers + provenance); the read side computes projections (badges, marks,\nsummaries, current staleness) at read time from typed fields. `answer_index`\nis a VIEW, never a materialized cache. `?version=` returns sealed artifacts;\nno version param returns latest + current projection — reads legitimately\ndiffer, so tests pin the version. [Seam D; ADR-0007/0014]\n\n**P7 · Aggregate with a single-writer transaction (Seam B).** All node/arrow\nwrites go through `graph`'s write API (`withGraphWrite` + `GraphWriter`) in\none transaction under a per-graph advisory lock. Invariants live at the\naggregate boundary: cycle refusal-and-redirect (layer 1), write-time recursive\nreachability (layer 2), pure-core typed error (layer 3) — all three exist.\n[Seam B; ADR-0005; 03 §8]\n\n**P8 · Strategy + Registry via resolution chains.** Every configurable\nbehaviour (operators accumulate/strict-and first among them) sits behind a\nstrategy interface; selection resolves parent → run → deployment through the\nregister, the supplying level is RECORDED on the produced number, and the\ndeployment row is mandatory (DR-074). A source-literal selection is a defect\nby definition. No silent defaults: `Unresolved` blocks visibly, the loader\nsubstitutes nothing. [ADR-0011; DR-074; H4; FX-PT-D2]\n\n**P9 · Pipeline of typed gates.** Serve is a fixed machine-ordered gate chain\n— R9 → Q53 → conformance → Q51, plus the DR-082/086 band-cap — where every\ngate returns a member of a closed outcome vocabulary and every terminal is a\nVALUE (components-only + DEFECT, downgrade, block), never an exception.\n`max_recompose = 2`; three compose-time routes to components-only; post-serve\neviction is a degradation, not a fourth route. [DR-049/057; DR-082/086; AC-52/53]\n\n**P10 · Append-only ledger + content-addressed identity.** Total order from a\ndedicated sequence allocator under a write lock (never timestamps). Named hash\ncolumns each hash one thing (`input_hash` excludes the contract hash;\n`contract_hash` is in cache identity); a cache hit yields a NEW row.\nImmutability is a database property: UPDATE and DELETE revoked + raising\ntriggers. [ADR-0006]\n\n**P11 · Transactional work queue behind a dispatcher (competing consumers +\nidempotent commands).** `core.work_item` claimed via\n`SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; the claim COMMITS BEFORE any model call; a\nwork item is an idempotent `(row, node-set)` command. The reaper writes\nexpiries; reads DERIVE expiry from `claim_deadline` without writing (AC-62 ×\nAC-89 — both halves, never a lazy write). The queue is never a provenance source.\n\n**`RULED — DR-118`: Hatchet dispatches this queue — it does not become it.**\nNine laws, and each one is the answer to a way the boundary erodes:\n\n1. **Assignment is not a claim.** Hatchet decides *which worker attempts this\n   now*; the claim is a **committed row in our database**, taken as the worker's\n   **first act inside any task**, before any model call. Never treat engine\n   assignment as the claim.\n2. **The first lines are THREE checks, not one** — open a short txn →\n   **settled-COMMAND check for this `(row, node-set)`, which no-ops regardless of\n   claim liveness** → claim (`SKIP LOCKED`) → COMMIT → only then the model. The\n   live-claim check alone closes the *concurrent* case; **the ledger-first check\n   closes the case where the work is done and the claim is long gone** (law 7:\n   the ledger, not the claim, is the source of record). **Scope it exactly**: it\n   fires on **command completion** — success or a terminal typed state that ends\n   the command — and **never on a retryable ledgered failure**. An attempt row\n   proves an attempt happened, not that the command finished; a typed failure\n   with budget remaining leaves the command unsettled, and **that is exactly what\n   a redelivered attempt is for**. Firing there strands the work permanently.\n3. **Settlement completion before the gateway — FINISH, DON'T REDO.** A claim is\n   not a licence to call. If the command already has a **durably-ledgered attempt\n   artifact awaiting settlement**, **complete the settlement from it** and exit;\n   if the **attempt ledger already shows the shared budget exhausted**, commit the\n   terminal exhausted state and exit. Neither ⇒ proceed — and a retryable failure\n   with budget remaining *is* \"neither\". This closes the window **between the\n   gateway's writes and the conditional settle**, where the command is not settled\n   and yet must not be re-called: *settled* and *completable* are different\n   questions. **Remaining budget comes from the attempt ledger, never from the\n   unsettled work-item row.**\n4. **`claim_deadline` must outlive the call it authorizes** — derived from the\n   call site's `CallBound.deadline` plus margin, both register values. **A claim\n   shorter than the call it authorizes is a defect in the register values, never\n   a licence for a second call.** Heartbeat-extension is the permitted\n   alternative where bounds vary (local vLLM latency).\n5. **The residual is named, not hidden.** A frozen worker is indistinguishable\n   from a dead one, so an expired claim **may** be re-claimed while a zombie\n   attempt is in flight and **two real calls may overlap**. Safe because:\n   attempt-scoped identities, **first settled wins with the loser RECORDED as a\n   superseded attempt** (never discarded — AC-44/AC-45), and **one attempt budget\n   counting both**. A duplicate is a cost event, **not** a DR-115 breach — both\n   artifacts are real; **discarding one would be** the breach.\n6. **Two expiry mechanisms compose, they do not compete.** Engine timeout →\n   re-dispatch; **our reaper** → expiry. The engine decides *when to try*; **our\n   claim and our ledger together** decide *whether work happens*.\n7. **Engine state is not the ledger and engine order is not `at_seq`.** Where they\n   disagree, ours wins. A dashboard is not an audit trail.\n8. **Model calls live in plain/child tasks, never naked in durable orchestrator\n   code** — replayed orchestrator code either re-issues the call or skips it, and\n   neither is acceptable. Orchestrators carry wait/spawn control flow only.\n9. **Engine retries are register values under DR-020's caps, never defaults** —\n   and they consume **the same attempt budget** as the gateway's, not an extra\n   one. Every attempt is a ledger row whoever initiated it.\n\n**And the transaction footgun is ours regardless of engine:** no `await` on the\nprovider gateway inside a transaction callback; the gateway's two writes take\ntheir own connection; a sibling's failure is caught, typed and ledgered per item,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"dfdcc576-cc73-4eef-a13b-94d8df416293","timestamp":"2026-08-08T13:05:46.867Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fa86b2af-8ec4-4964-b400-9f0c32601675","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{"parentUuid":"9bd86e0e-c9b2-45e6-8edc-878e39d45196","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1b24d198-6edd-416c-934d-0ab6c293f093","agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01F5etNyQHPsoRmohAnj6RL1","type":"tool_result","content":"**P12 · Algebraic data types everywhere.** Closed vocabularies are minted once\n(spec §12.3 → transcribed once into `kernel`), modeled as discriminated\nunions with compile-time exhaustive switches + one runtime schema + a Postgres\nCHECK. **Exhaustiveness needs both halves**: the compiler decides a switch is\nexhaustive *when there is a fall-through to decide against*, so the\n`require-exhaustive-switch` lint must also assert **the fall-through exists** — a\nswitch with none is silently non-exhaustive and the compiler has nothing to\ncomplain about (03 §6.3). Branded identity types; the labeled-number value object\n(number + kind + source + producer + replay handle) is the ONLY number shape that\ncrosses the wire. Unknown member ⇒ loud typed failure, never coercion.\n[ADR-0001; AC-35/63/65]\n\n**P13 · Memento / deterministic replay.** The evaluation snapshot is\nimmutable; the arrow order is derived once (`graph.materialiseSnapshot`),\nRECORDED on `propagation_run` (`NULLS FIRST` explicit), and every\nrecomputation CONSUMES the recorded order — nobody re-derives. Every ruled\nstructural outcome (lift markers, cluster records, operator + level,\ntransmission reductions, selection rule) lands as recorded columns, or the\nceremony proves nothing. [ADR-0004/0012]\n\n**P14 · Observer via SSE with declared consumers.** One stream on the same\nfront door; closed event vocabulary in `packages/contract`; E1 — no event\nwithout a declared consumer (orphan audit enforces); E2 — one name per\nmeaning; payloads projection-grade or bare signals, never bundle-grade.\nDR-076's lifecycle events follow these laws. [ADR-0008; DR-076]\n\n**P15 · Bulkhead failure isolation.** A panel member's failure isolates to a\ntyped note — never fails the primary run; dispersion needs ≥2 judgements or\nis typed absent (never zero). Every caught failure is typed AND ledgered — a\nswallowed exception is a defect. [AC-04; FX-LG-15; charter A3]\n\n**P16 · Shadow mode (dark launch) for gates.** A gate whose policy V has not\nfilled runs tier-invariant in shadow: it PUBLISHES what it would have\nsuppressed beside the unsuppressed result (`shadow_suppression`). Register-\ngated branches must be exercisable in BOTH states by a producible\nconfiguration (G4); a gate the pack says does not ship is NOT WRITTEN\n(attestation instead — never dormant code). [DR-085; DR-081; DR-088]\n\n**P17 · DDL as the single authority.** Invariants live in hand-authored SQL\ninside the migration that creates the table — **`drizzle-kit` migrations under\n`RULED — DR-117`, with Drizzle declaring the tables**; application checks are\ncourtesy restatements. **A schema-diffing generator is a drafting aid, NEVER the\nauthority** — it will not see the CHECK expressions, triggers, grants or\npartial-index predicates it did not itself emit, so a builder who trusts it\nsilently drops the exact layer AC-32 lives in. Fixtures insert through the **RAW\nconnection**, bypassing every validator. Null-safety is explicit (`NOT NULL` +\nCHECK — a bare btrim CHECK accepts NULL). Upserts distinguish collapse (identical\npayload → no-op) from typed integrity error (differing payload) — not expressible\nas a unique index. **The durable-execution engine is a co-tenant on the instance,\nnever a co-owner of the lineage**: its schema is not ours, not in `02`, and **no\nV3 query joins across the boundary** (ADR-0003 rule 4). [ADR-0003 rules 1–4;\nADR-0005]\n\n**P18 · Attestations as first-class artifacts.** Receipts and attestations\n(independence receipt, operator attestation, NOT-SHIPPED, maker-capability)\nare typed, falsifiable artifacts assembled into the acceptance bundle — an\nabsence is a typed reason, never a default (\"independence unknown\" ≠\n\"independent\"). [VR-3; charter §5.2; AC-38]\n\n## The anti-pattern register (rejected by ruling — do not introduce)\n\n- **SCAFFOLDED / DEMO DATA ON A RUNTIME PATH — `RULED — DR-115`, and the newest\n  entry here.** V's standing law: *\"the algorithm never fabricates runtime data —\n  every judgement, composition, evidence item, score and served artifact in any run\n  comes from REAL model calls, real retrieval and real computation.\"* No stubbed\n  judge responses, no hardcoded sample debates, no seeded artifacts masquerading as\n  generation, no demo data on production paths, **and no fixture used as a fallback\n  when a call fails** — an unjudged node emits no arrow and a typed record (P-D1),","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"615254e5-5fde-4891-a7d2-43af6bb884eb","timestamp":"2026-08-08T13:05:55.351Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9bd86e0e-c9b2-45e6-8edc-878e39d45196","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

<= input.maxRecompose; attempt += 1) {\n263\t    const composed = await dependencies.compose(input.factBundle, attempt);\n264\t    const nodeIds = new Set(input.nodes.map((node) => node.nodeId));\n265\t    const loadBearingNodeIds = new Set(input.nodes.filter((node) => node.loadBearing).map((node) => node.nodeId));\n266\t    if (composed.some((segment) => segment.assertedNodeRefs.some((nodeRef) => !nodeIds.has(nodeRef)))) {\n267\t      throw new TypedDomainError(\"COMPOSITION_CONTRACT_ERROR\", \"A composed segment references a node outside the serve set\");\n268\t    }\n269\t    segments = composed.map((segment) => Object.freeze({\n270\t      ...segment,\n271\t      loadBearing: segment.servedNumberRefs.length > 0\n272\t        || unique\");\n281\t    }\n282\t    trace.push(attempt === 1 ? \"COMPOSED\" : \"RECOMPOSED_ONCE\");\n283\t    coverageMode = input.strangerSampleRate >= 1 ? \"EXHAUSTIVE\" : \"SAMPLED\";\n284\t    conformance = await Promise.all(segments.map(async (segment) => {\n285\t      if (segment.loadBearing) return dependencies.conform(segment, \"JUDGED\");\n286\t      if (input.strangerSampleRate >= 1 || dependencies.selectSample(segment, input.strangerSampleRate)) {\n287\t        return dependencies.conform(segment, \"SAMPLED_PASSED\");\n288\t      }\n289\t      return { segmentId: segment.segmentId, state: \"NOT_SAMPLED\", conforms: true } as const;\n290\t    }));\n291\t    if (conformance.every((judgement) => judgement.conforms)) {\n292\t      trace.push(coverageMode === \"EXHAUSTIVE\"\n293\t        ? \"GATE3_CONFORMANCE_PASS_EXHAUSTIVE\"\n294\t        : \"GATE3_CONFORMANCE_PASS_SAMPLED\");\n295\t      break;\n296\t    }\n297\t    trace.push(\"GATE3_CONFORMANCE_FAIL\");\n298\t  }\n299\t\n300\t  if (!conformance.every((judgement) => judgement.conforms)) {\n301\t    trace.push(\"COMPONENTS_ONLY_DEFECT\");\n302\t    return componentsOnly(input, trace, segments, conformance, coverageMode);\n303\t  }\n304\t\n305\t  let terminal: ServeGateResult[\"terminal\"];\n306\t  let answerForm: AnswerForm;\n307\t  const loadBearingNodes = input.nodes.filter((node) => node.loadBearing);\n308\t  if (loadBearingNodes.some((node) => node.wayOfKnowing === \"LOOKED_UP\" && node.locator === null)) {\n309\t    trace.push(\"GATE4_Q51_LOCATOR_BLOCK\", \"COMPONENTS_ONLY_DEFECT\");\n310\t    return componentsOnly(input, trace, segments, conformance, coverageMode);\n311\t  }\n312\t  if (loadBearingNodes.every((node) => node.wayOfKnowing === \"REASONING\")) {\n313\t    if (segments.length < 2 || segments[0] === undefined || segments[1] === undefined) {\n314\t      throw new TypedDomainError(\n315\t        \"COMPOSITION_CONTRACT_ERROR\",\n316\t        \"A reasoning answer requires both a hypothesis and a research-plan segment\"\n317\t      );\n318\t    }\n319\t    trace.push(\"GATE4_Q51_DOWNGRADE\");\n320\t    terminal = \"DOWNGRADED\";\n321\t    answerForm = {\n322\t      kind: \"HYPOTHESIS_WITH_RESEARCH_PLAN\",\n323\t      hypothesis: segments[0].text,\n324\t      researchPlan: segments[1].text\n325\t    };\n326\t  } else {\n327\t    trace.push(\"GATE4_Q51_PASS\");\n328\t    terminal = \"SERVED\";\n329\t    answerForm = { kind: \"VERDICT\", text: input.factBundle.buildsOnPrevious\n357\t    }\n358\t  };\n359\t}\n360\t\n361\tconst SERVE_ITEM_STATUSES = [\"READY\", \"PENDING\", \"ERROR\"] as const;\n362\ttype ServeItemStatus = typeof  }\n378\t  if (!Array.isArray(input.items)) {\n379\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"SERVE_ITEMS_NOT_A_LIST\", \"Serve items must be a list\");\n380\t  }\n381\t  for (const item of input.items) The expected flow has R9 node text pre-composition first, followed by Q53 and conformance checks, with post-composition steps and band capping applied afterward. Looking at the actual implementation in runServeGateChain, I can see R9 node processing blocks first, then Q53 blocks, and the composition budget check follows with its own blocking conditions.\n\nNow I'm working through the remaining gates: the compose loop runs conformance checks up to the recompose limit, Q51 applies locator blocking or reasoning-only downgrades, post-compose R9 runs its own verdict check, and finally the band ceiling applies the cap. The key question is whether post-compose R9 runs after Q51 as the spec indicates, since Q51 is described as the last gate in the pre-compose order and post-compose R9 is an additional pass on the composed verdict. Now I'm checking the Q51 locator gate requirement — the spec says every load-bearing claim needs its kind, producer, and locator, with a missing locator blocking serving. I'm verifying whether the implementation correctly restricts this check to only LOOKED_UP nodes that lack locators, or if it should apply more broadly across all claim types.\n\nI'm also cross-referencing the WayOfKnowing vocabulary in the kernel to confirm the three kinds (looked-up, ran, reasoned) and checking whether RAN nodes should use the run handle as their locator while REASONING nodes might need different handling. There's also the conformance outcome logic to consider — ensuring exhaustive coverage stays exhaustive without sampling — and I need to verify how coverageMode gets derived from the stranger sample rate threshold.\n\nThe segment derivation is correctly typed-ref-based, overriding any asserted load-bearing flags through the spread operator, which aligns with the rule that a segment is load-bearing if it references a served number or asserts facts from a load-bearing node. Now I'm tracking down the actual BandCeiling implementation and its numeric source, since it's dependency-injected in serve/index.ts but I need to find where it's actually defined and what feeds its values.\n\nThere's also a critical ambiguity in DR-049: when maxRecompose = 2, does that mean 2 recompose attempts (3 total compositions) or 2 total compose attempts? The loop structure suggests 2 total attempts, but I need to verify what the spec actually intends for the components-only fallback trigger. The trace correctly marks the second attempt as RECOMPOSED_ONCE, which aligns with the serve_state vocabulary. Now I'm moving on to how terminals are represented as values.\n\nThe `runServeGateChain` function returns explicit values for BLOCK and COMPONENTS_ONLY states, but throws TypedDomainError for contract violations like empty node sets, mismatched recompose counts, unresolved budgets, invalid measurements, composition failures, or incomplete reasoning. These are programming errors rather than gate terminals, which fits the pattern of loud typed failures for unknown or invalid states.\n\nLooking at each named terminal, they all map to values: R9 and Q53 blocks return BLOCKED, budget and conformance failures return COMPONENTS_ONLY, Q51 locator returns COMPONENTS_ONLY, Q51 reasoning returns DOWNGRADED, post-compose R9 returns COMPONENTS_ONLY, and band cap returns SERVED with a cap. But there's a wrinkle—the reasoning-only downgrade throws when the composition returns fewer than two segments, which converts what should be a gate terminal path into an exception. That's technically a composition contract error, not a gate terminal value.\n\nThe real concern is that a legitimate reasoning-only answer could produce a single segment from composition, triggering a throw instead of serving a terminal. S05 serve pipeline hardening: sealed artifacts plus append-only read overlays.\n2\t\n3\t-- Forward-apply S04 review parity for databases that already recorded 0005.\n4\tCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION register.claim_type_composition_map_is_valid(candidate jsonb)\n5\tRETURNS boolean\n6\tLANGUAGE plpgsql\n7\tIMMUTABLE\n8\tPARALLEL SAFE\n9\tAS $$\n10\tDECLARE\n11\t  member_key text;\n12\t  member jsonb;\n13\t  item jsonb;\n14\tBEGIN\n15\t  IF jsonb_typeof(candidate) <> 'object'\n16\t    OR candidate ->> 'kind' <> 'CLAIM_TYPE_COMPOSITION_MAP'\n17\t    OR jsonb_typeof(candidate -> 'entries') <> 'object'\n18\t    OR candidate - ARRAY['kind', 'entries']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n19\t  THEN\n20\t    RETURN false;\n21\t  END IF;\n22\t\n23\t  FOR member_key, member IN SELECT key, value FROM jsonb_each(candidate -> 'entries')\n24\t  LOOP\n25\t    IF member_key NOT IN ('empirical', 'causal', 'normative', 'definitional', 'prediction', 'comparative', 'mixed', 'unknown')\n26\t      OR jsonb_typeof(member) <> 'object'\n27\t      OR NOT member ?& ARRAY['branch', 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity', 'terms', 'caps', 'uncertaintyLadder']\n28\t      OR member - ARRAY['branch', 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity', 'terms', 'caps', 'uncertaintyLadder']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n29\t      OR member ->> 'branch' NOT IN ('EVIDENCE_AWARE', 'EVIDENCE_FREE')\n30\t      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity') <> 'number'\n31\t      OR (member ->> 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity')::numeric NOT BETWEEN 0 AND 1\n32\t      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'terms') <> 'array'\n33\t      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'caps') <> 'array'\n34\t      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'uncertaintyLadder') <> 'array'\n35\t    THEN\n36\t      RETURN false;\n37\t    END IF;\n38\t\n39\t    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'terms')\n40\t    LOOP\n41\t      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n42\t        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['metric', 'coefficient']\n43\t        OR item - ARRAY['metric', 'coefficient']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n44\t        OR item ->> 'metric' NOT IN ('steelman_fidelity', 'counter_resilience', 'evidence_quality', 'evidence_relevance', 'context_fit', 'clarity', 'fallacy_resilience')\n45\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'coefficient') <> 'number'\n46\t        OR (item ->> 'coefficient')::numeric NOT BETWEEN 0 AND 1\n47\t      THEN\n48\t        RETURN false;\n49\t      END IF;\n50\t    END LOOP;\n51\t\n52\t    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'caps')\n53\t    LOOP\n54\t      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n55\t        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['whenFatalType', 'to', 'why', 'by']\n56\t        OR item - ARRAY['whenFatalType', 'to', 'why', 'by']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n57\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'whenFatalType') <> 'string'\n58\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'to') <> 'number'\n59\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'why') <> 'string'\n60\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'by') <> 'string'\n61\t        OR length(btrim(item ->> 'whenFatalType')) = 0\n62\t        OR (item ->> 'to')::numeric NOT BETWEEN 0 AND 1\n63\t        OR length(btrim(item ->> 'why')) = 0\n64\t        OR length(btrim(item ->> 'by')) = 0\n65\t      THEN\n66\t        RETURN false;\n67\t      END IF;\n68\t    END LOOP;\n69\t\n70\t    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'uncertaintyLadder')\n71\t    LOOP\n72\t      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n73\t        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['atMost', 'label']\n74\t        OR item - ARRAY['atMost', 'label']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n75\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'atMost') <> 'number'\n76\t        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'label') <> 'string'\n77\t        OR (item ->> 'atMost')::numeric NOT BETWEEN 0 AND 1\n78\t        OR length(btrim(item ->> 'label')) = 0\n79\t      THEN\n80\t        RETURN false;\n81\t      END IF;\n82\t    END LOOP;\n83\t  END LOOP;\n84\t  RETURN true;\n85\tEXCEPTION WHEN others THEN\n86\t  RETURN false;\n87\tEND;\n88\t$$;\n89\t\n90\tALTER TABLE register.register_row\n91\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS register_row_claim_type_composition_map_shape,\n92\t  ADD CONSTRAINT register_row_claim_type_composition_map_shape CHECK (\n93\t    row_key <> 'claimTypeCompositionMap'\n94\t    OR register.claim_type_composition_map_is_valid(value_json)\n95\t  );\n96\t\n97\tALTER TABLE serve.answer\n98\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS confidence_band text,\n99\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS band_ceiling jsonb,\n100\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS reversal_point text,\n101\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS builds_on_previous jsonb,\n102\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS badges jsonb,\n103\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS verdict_unavailable jsonb;\n104\t\n105\tALTER TABLE serve.served_number\n106\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS answer_id uuid,\n107\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS answer_version integer,\n108\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS number_ref text;\n109\t\n110\tALTER TABLE serve.served_number_event\n111\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS reason text;\n112\t\n113\tCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION serve.conformance_segment_results_are_valid(candidate jsonb)\n114\tRETURNS boolean\n115\tLANGUAGE plpgsql\n116\tIMMUTABLE\n117\tPARALLEL SAFE\n118\tAS $$\n119\tBEGIN\n120\t  IF jsonb_typeof(candidate) <> 'array' THEN\n121\t    RETURN false;\n122\t  END IF;\n123\t  RETURN NOT EXISTS (\n124\t    SELECT 1\n125\t    FROM jsonb_array_elements(candidate) AS segment\n126\t    WHERE jsonb_typeof(segment) <> 'object'\n127\t       OR segment ->> 'state' NOT IN ('JUDGED', 'SAMPLED_PASSED', 'NOT_SAMPLED')\n128\t       OR jsonb_typeof(segment -> 'segmentId') <> 'string'\n129\t       OR jsonb_typeof(segment -> 'conforms') <> 'boolean'\n130\t  );\n131\tEXCEPTION WHEN others THEN\n132\t  RETURN false;\n133\tEND;\n134\t$$;\n135\t\n136\tALTER TABLE serve.served_number_event\n137\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS served_number_event_reason_matches_status,\n138\t  ADD CONSTRAINT served_number_event_reason_matches_status CHECK (\n139\t    (status = 'PRESENT' AND reason IS NULL)\n140\t    OR (status = 'EVICTED' AND reason = 'MISSING-NUMBER')\n141\t    OR (status = 'WITHHELD' AND reason = 'STRICT_AND_CONJUNCT_UNJUDGED_OR_ABSTAINED')\n142\t  ) NOT VALID;\n143\t\n144\tALTER TABLE serve.conformance_record\n145\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS conformance_record_segment_result_shape,\n146\t  ADD CONSTRAINT conformance_record_segment_result_shape\n147\t    CHECK (serve.conformance_segment_results_are_valid(segment_results)) NOT VALID;\n148\t\n149\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS serve.segment_suppression (\n150\t  answer_id uuid NOT NULL,\n151\t  answer_version integer NOT NULL,\n152\t  segment_id text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(segment_id)) > 0),\n153\t  evicted_number_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(evicted_number_ref)) > 0),\n154\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0),\n155\t  PRIMARY KEY (answer_id, answer_version, segment_id, evicted_number_ref),\n156\t  FOREIGN KEY (answer_id, answer_version)\n157\t    REFERENCES serve.answer(answer_id, answer_version)\n158\t);\n159\t\n160\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS serve.condition_mark (\n161\t  condition_mark_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n162\t  answer_id uuid NOT NULL,\n163\t  answer_version integer NOT NULL,\n164\t  mark text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(mark)) > 0),\n165\t  scope text NOT NULL CHECK (scope IN ('answer', 'node')),\n166\t  subject_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(subject_ref)) > 0),\n167\t  reason text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(reason)) > 0),\n168\t  lift_path text,\n169\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0),\n170\t  FOREIGN KEY (answer_id, answer_version)\n171\t    REFERENCES serve.answer(answer_id, answer_version)\n172\t);\n173\t\n174\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS serve.condition_mark_node (\n175\t  condition_mark_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES serve.condition_mark(condition_mark_id),\n176\t  node_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.node(node_id),\n177\t  PRIMARY KEY (condition_mark_id, node_id)\n178\t);\n179\t\n180\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS serve.shadow_suppression (\n181\t  shadow_suppression_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n182\t  answer_id uuid NOT NULL,\n183\t  answer_version integer NOT NULL,\n184\t  gate text NOT NULL CHECK (gate IN ('EVIDENCE_GATE', 'VALUE_OVERLAY')),\n185\t  subject_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(subject_ref)) > 0),\n186\t  would_have_suppressed jsonb NOT NULL,\n187\t  unlock_condition text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(unlock_condition)) > 0),\n188\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0),\n189\t  FOREIGN KEY (answer_id, answer_version)\n190\t    REFERENCES serve.answer(answer_id, answer_version)\n191\t);\n192\t\n193\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS serve.abstention (\n194\t  abstention_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n195\t  answer_id uuid NOT NULL,\n196\t  answer_version integer NOT NULL,\n197\t  kind text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(kind)) > 0),\n198\t  question_class text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(question_class)) > 0),\n199\t  risk_tier text NOT NULL CHECK (risk_tier IN ('casual', 'standard', 'high-stakes')),\n200\t  price double precision NOT NULL CHECK (price > 0 AND price < 1),\n201\t  register_row_key text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(register_row_key)) > 0),\n202\t  register_version bigint NOT NULL CHECK (register_version > 0),\n203\t  register_source_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(register_source_ref)) > 0),\n204\t  unlock_condition text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(unlock_condition)) > 0),\n205\t  ledger_unknown_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(ledger_unknown_ref)) > 0),\n206\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0),\n207\t  FOREIGN KEY (answer_id, answer_version)\n208\t    REFERENCES serve.answer(answer_id, answer_version)\n209\t);\n210\t\n211\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS segment_suppression_answer_version\n212\t  ON serve.segment_suppression(answer_id, answer_version, at_seq);\n213\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS shadow_suppression_answer_version\n214\t  ON serve.shadow_suppression(answer_id, answer_version, at_seq);\n215\t\n216\tGRANT SELECT, INSERT ON serve.segment_suppression, serve.condition_mark,\n217\t  serve.condition_mark_node, serve.shadow_suppression, serve.abstention TO debateai_runtime;\n218\tGRANT SELECT ON serve.segment_suppression, serve.condition_mark,\n219\t  serve.condition_mark_node, serve.shadow_suppression, serve.abstention TO debateai_replay;\n220\tREVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON serve.segment_suppression, serve.condition_mark,\n221\t  serve.condition_mark_node, serve.shadow_suppression, serve.abstention FROM PUBLIC, debateai_runtime;\n222\t\n223\tDO $$\n224\tDECLARE table_name text;\n225\tBEGIN\n226\t  FOREACH table_name IN ARRAY ARRAY[\n227\t    'serve.segment_suppression', 'serve.condition_mark',\n228\t    'serve.condition_mark_node', 'serve.shadow_suppression', 'serve.abstention'\n229\t  ] LOOP\n230\t    EXECUTE format('DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS reject_mutation ON %s', table_name);\n231\t    EXECUTE format(\n232\t      'CREATE TRIGGER reject_mutation BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE ON %s FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION {"parentUuid":"8498e29e-df03-435c-90d4-355919942e9c","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdqLMzXdkJVhMAa2kt9Vk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The idempotency test runs `migrate()` twice, and the second call skips everything due to the ledger, so it only validates the ledger list itself. The actual failure was the stale ledger expectation—missing the 0006_s05.sql entry—which failed on array comparison, not on any DDL replay error. But the orchestrator flagged it as a non-replay-safe DDL issue under the applied-migrations ledger test, even though mechanically `migrate` skips those already-applied migrations, so the DDL shouldn't actually execute.\n\nThe worker's handoff clarified that Rework 1 updated the stale expected ledger list through 0006 and added the migration-class lint check. So the lint is a text-level gate that catches non-replay-safe patterns—a genuine hygiene improvement—but the actual failure mechanism was just the stale list. {"parentUuid":"0d4241fa-b5c2-45a8-a13e-0b8ba093c95e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdqLSWjCG3kQ9aVG5FYWL","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'm noticing a significant security vulnerability in how the API handles operator authentication. The `resolveSession` function accepts any non-empty string in the `x-operator-dev-token` header without actually validating it against a registered or environment-configured token, which means the operator-only inspection route is essentially open to anyone. This is different from how asker tokens work, where the token is used to derive a specific asker ID.\n\nThe real problem is that the inspection endpoint doesn't scope results by any operator identity or asker ownership — it just returns the tier-2 structured inspection for whatever answer ID is requested. So while the route claims to require an operator token, since that token is never verified, any caller can read inspection data for any answer. {"parentUuid":"48d21256-0712-4d0a-9d95-2c32b1fe6ed3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"fbf967a2-c1fb-43f2-a43f-5f0708dcb701","agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JTphgRwhiwyJHX7e13zWnm","type":"tool_result","content":"106:  it(\"FX-WIRE-01 keeps the inspection facet operator-scoped and raw-output-free\", async () => {\n110:      url: \"/v1/answers/answer:test/inspection?version=1\",\n115:    const operator = await api.inject({\n117:      url: \"/v1/answers/answer:test/inspection?version=1\",\n118:      headers: { \"x-operator-dev-token\": \"operator-token\" }\n120:    expect(operator.statusCode).toBe(200);\n121:    expect(operator.body).not.toContain(\"raw_text\");\n122:    expect(operator.json()).toMatchObject({ answer_id: \"answer:test\", answer_version: 1 });","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"42f90b53-4426-4628-850d-9e0661784717","timestamp":"2026-08-08T13:10:15.165Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"48d21256-0712-4d0a-9d95-2c32b1fe6ed3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}
{"parentUuid":"42f90b53-4426-4628-850d-9e0661784717","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a9cf8c6f0027c36d4","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdqLTxPp8by3DF4xzmbMN","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'm noticing a critical mismatch between the API contract specification and the actual implementation. The disclosure table marks the `/v1/answers/{id}/inspection` endpoint as tier 2 with access for the asker, but the code is enforcing operator-only access and rejecting asker requests with a 403 error—essentially inverting the intended audience.\n\nLooking more closely at FX-WIRE-01, the constraint isn't that the inspection route itself is operator-only; it's specifically that raw_text payloads are operator-only. The inspection resource should be accessible to tier 2 (the asker), but without exposing the raw model call transcripts. And FX-WIRE-03 confirms the tier-2 inspection gate is evaluated against session ownership—the asker is the principal for this tier.\n\nThe current implementation has three critical issues: it's rejecting the asker with a 403 when they should have access, it's not applying any ownership scoping to the inspection read, and it allows any asker to escalate to operator scope by reusing their token in the operator header, which would let them read any answer's inspection data across tenants. This is a blocking security finding that violates the disclosure tier and creates cross-tenant exposure.\n\nI'm verifying the code path — the inspection route discards the session parameter entirely and the underlying SQL query has no asker join, unlike the answer and node projections which both filter by asker ID. This confirms the inspection endpoint is missing the ownership scoping that's present everywhere else. The route itself appears to be new in this sprint, so this is a fresh vulnerability.\n\nI should check whether the contract definitions have been regenerated, but running the generation script would modify files on disk, which violates the read-only constraint. The handoff indicates the build passes, so I'll trust that for now.\n\nLooking at the test coverage: AC-53 route 3 and DR-078 are confirmed tested. For the sealed-read behavior with `?version=`, I'm tracing through how `readAnswerProjection` handles the version parameter — when a version is provided, it forces the status to \"PRESENT\" for all numbers rather than using the current status.\n\nBut that seems problematic. WITHHELD is a seal-time state (part of the strict-AND conjunction), not something that should be overwritten on a sealed read. Only EVICTED is the post-serve degradation that gets ignored when reading a sealed artifact. Forcing WITHHELD to PRESENT would fabricate a number that was never actually served, which violates the seal semantics.\n\nActually, let me check if this is even reachable. Looking at the runtime, `persist()` only writes PRESENT events and `recordReplayEviction` writes EVICTED — WITHHELD is never actually produced during execution. So this defect is latent, not live, though it's still worth noting as a potential issue.\n\nBut stepping back: the sealed-read intent is to return sealed text and PRESENT numbers according to the handoff spec. The conformance record should be byte-identical before and after eviction, and the replay ceremony reads without the overlay. So the sealed read should ignore post-seal eviction events, but WITHHELD isn't an overlay — it's part of the sealed state itself. The correct approach is to fold on the first event at seal time, not blanket all events as PRESENT.\n\nThere's also a code duplication issue: `deriveAnswerServeState` handles SEALED_VERSION correctly in the pure function layer, but `readAnswerProjection` reimplements the same logic inline in SQL/TypeScript instead of reusing those tested functions. This means the actual served path diverges from the tested path — `foldServedNumberEvents` and `deriveAnswerServeState` are effectively untested in production, which is a real functional correctness risk.\n\nI need to search for where these Now I need to examine lines 610-690 of the integration test to verify the sealed-versus-current read assertions and understand how the runner generates terminal DOWNGRADED states with REASONING wayOfKnowing on a single node.\n\nThe issue is that the CAPPED branch only fires when capApplies is true, but the runner's actual band-ceiling application never exercises this path — it's only tested in the unit test with a stubbed dependency. Since the band cap is a core requirement of the ticket, the production code path that applies the cap has zero coverage. The configuration to trigger it is producible (just flip capApplies to true), but no test actually does this. This violates the principle that register-gated branches must be exercisable in both states through a producible configuration. Combined with the deeper problem that the runner's applyBandCeiling doesn't use the basis for its decision, I think this warrants flagging as a BLOCKING coverage gap.\n\nBut stepping back, the real issue is that the decision function itself is missing. The ruled law requires the ceiling label to be selected from the distribution based on the basis value, but the implementation replaced that logic with a static config boolean. This means the ceiling label never changes regardless of whether the answer comes from reasoning or lookup — it's the same static label every time, and the cap fires identically for every answer. That's not implementing the decision function at all.\n\nThe composition budget works the right way: it measures the bundle, compares it against a register-supplied bound, and terminates if exceeded. Only the bound value comes from configuration. The band cap should follow that same pattern — compute the distribution, compare against register-supplied cut points, select the ceiling label from the ordered vocabulary, and cap when the candidate band exceeds it. Instead, the entire comparison got collapsed into a boolean flag.\n\nThis needs to be a blocking finding because the ruled decision mechanism for the band-cap gate isn't actually implemented.\n\nBut wait — to compute whether the candidate band exceeds the ceiling, I'd need the ordered band label vocabulary from the register. To determine which ceiling label the distribution earns, I'd need the cut-point matrix from the register. Both are still pending configuration values. However, the code can accept them as typed register inputs and fail loudly when they're missing, exactly like `compositionBudget.bound` does. So this is a real gap, not a forced deferral — it's buildable now.\n\nThis is blocking work. It's the third pillar of the ticket and the charter-level honesty surface for how we know the ceiling. Looking back at the build order, the rule itself is marked as ruled at S05, while the values for the tier list, mapping tables, and cut points defer to VG-02. The mechanism is owed now, values later. So blocking stands.\n\nI need to Three wire consequences:\n\n- **`band_ceiling` is non-optional on every Answer** (§8.9). An answer whose band\n  names no ceiling is an answer that broke charter VR-2's *\"every band names its\n  ceiling\"*, and optionality would let a serializer do the omitting.\n- **A capped answer is an ordinary served answer** — no new `serve_state` member,\n  no terminal route, no error. §13.4's \"deliberately not an error\" list holds.\n- **The label vocabulary and the cut points remain register rows V ratifies at\n  DR-023**, printed per AC-75. **This document still states no value**\n  (AC-74, AC-76).\n\nThis is M4's surviving carrier once `OD-12` removed the numeric τ ceiling, and\nDR-082/086 are what make it a gate rather than a decoration. **The fixture is\n`06-test-strategy.md`'s `FX-SRV-13`** — *the band cap: a second, independent gate\nthat caps and serves* — at **S5**.\n\n### 9.6 Edges carry a polymorphic target\n\n`{target_kind: NODE, target_node_id}` or `{target_kind: EDGE, target_edge_id}`\n(AC-19; Plan.md §5.4, §6.6 UI-2). That's clearly BLOCKING.\n\nThe third finding is that eight exported serve functions exist but have no production callers, they're not in the orphan-audit inventory, and the handoff documentation implies they should be in the pipeline — that's another BLOCKING-level gap.\n\nActually, reconsidering the third one: maybe I'm being too strict. The precedent from S04 allowed unattached surfaces as long as they were declared in the orphan audit, but here they're not declared at all. The fix is straightforward though, so I'll keep it BLOCKING but frame it as needing either declaration or attachment. I need to verify whether `verdict_state` is new in S05 or carried forward from earlier — the column existed at S00 and the serve persist path writes it, but I can't diff to be sure. Given that uncertainty about origin, I'll mark it BLOCKING but note that if it predates S05, it needs treatment as a carry-forward issue.\n\nActually, AC-88 explicitly states \"status is derived, never asserted\" in S5's acceptance criteria, so asserting a served verdict_state is squarely a violation of that. That makes it BLOCKING. Three BLOCKING findings feels like a reasonable outcome for a first pass on a large slice. So I'm settling on: the `/inspection` tier-2 audience inversion with no ownership scoping and header scope elevation, the band cap where DR-082/086 decisions weren't implemented, and the verdict_state assertion.\n\nFor non-blocking issues, there's the sealed-read version parameter that fabricates a number never actually served, and the band_ceiling/confidence_band pairing that lacks proper DDL or wire invariants despite the charter requiring every band to name its ceiling.\n\nThe real duplication problem is that `foldServedNumberEvents` and `deriveAnswerServeState` are reimplementing read-path logic that's already baked into the SQL and TypeScript inline — two separate implementations of the same rule. The migration linter only covers four specific ordered classes, leaving bare CREATE TABLE and CREATE TRIGGER statements unguarded in migrations 0002 and 0003, though the text lint is now the primary safeguard. Finally, the contract test's \"parity\" check is just string-containment matching against SQL rather than actual behavioral parity between the implementations.\n\nThere's also a growing list of structural issues: pg_dump can't handle UDFs in CHECK constraints, so adding a second instance in migration 0006 expands the ceremony surface instead of keeping it flat; verdict-R9's position after Q51 isn't constrained by any ruled row; the `serve_state` wire enum has picked up a fourth member `BLOCKED` that contradicts the three-member contract in the API spec; and under exhaustive sampling, non-load-bearing segments are being handled inconsistently.\n\nTrimming down to the meaningful ones—keeping around eight non-blocking issues. On the verdict lines: SOLID looks mostly good despite the runner's large closures and execute method, and the gate chain's dependency inversion is clean, though pushing the band-cap decision entirely out of the domain is where the rule got lost—I'll greenlight it with a note. DDD is next to evaluate. The test that asserts the wrong behavior is a TDD-quality issue rooted in misreading the law, but the underlying discipline is sound, so I'll greenlight TDD and flag it. For Patterns, I'm rejecting the register — the band-cap gate isn't actually a gate, the unfilled-policy gate should run in shadow or refuse rather than accept a boolean verdict, and there's an extra `serve_state` member on the wire. DR-115 looks good though — no fabricated runtime data on the new paths and provider calls are genuine.\n\nI'm also noticing that `runServeGateChain` doesn't validate the BandCeilingDecision fields it receives, so blanks could slip through and only get caught by the wire schema after persistence — that's worth adding to the findings.
